## 5 - Risk Register

| 5 - Risk Register  |  VPCS – Virelan Protected Communications System (3 x GEO Protected SatCom) |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
|  | # | ID | Risk Category | Risk Sub-category | Risk Name | Type of Risk | Description | Internal Factors | External Factors | Probability | Mitigation Strategy | Mitigation Strategy Narrative | Cost Impact – Optimistic ($000) | Cost Impact – Most Likely ($000) | Cost Impact – Pessimistic ($000) | Weighted Exposure ($000) | Cost Impact in Phase | Schedule Impact in Phase | Impact on Project | Impact on Analysis |
|  | 1 | R-001 | Spectrum & Regulatory | Spectrum Coordination | Ka-Band Coordination Incomplete for 40°, 160°, 280°E Slots | Governance | International coordination of the 20/30 GHz assignments for the three GEO slots is not completed before bringing-into-use, or imposes constraints that reduce usable beam capacity. | Late filing; incomplete traffic model; limited spectrum staff | Neighbouring operators' prior filings; frequencies 'subject to assignment' (Part A) | 0.45 | Mitigate | File coordination requests in Phase A (Sep 2027), pre-negotiate with adjacent-slot operators and keep channelization reconfigurable. | 1500 | 6000 | 18000 | 2700 | A–C | A–E | Capacity reduction or delayed service start. | Affects payload design and operations assumptions. |
|  | 2 | R-002 | Spacecraft & Payload Technical | Antenna & Reflector Deployment | Deployable Reflector Deployment Failure | Technical | One of the two deployable reflectors on a spacecraft fails to deploy or latch after GEO arrival, removing up to four of the eight spot beams on that spacecraft. | Hold-down release mechanism design; harness snag; limited deployment cycles under gravity offload | First-deployment thermal environment; launch shock from three separate campaigns | 0.12 | Mitigate | Qualify the release mechanism with margin, perform deployment tests before and after environmental testing on every flight unit, and use redundant release actuators. | 8000 | 45000 | 160000 | 5400 | C–LEOP | LEOP–E | Permanent loss of up to 50% of one spacecraft's beams. | Drives reflector qualification cost and the mission-success scenario. |
|  | 3 | R-003 | Security & Cryptography | Key Management | Key-Management and Recovery Architecture Immature | Security | The key-management system cannot demonstrate ≤24 h rotation of 256-bit session keys and immediate authenticated revocation across three spacecraft and four gateways by PDR. | Late security requirements; crypto boundary defined after bus architecture; small COMSEC team | Security-authority approval cycles; evolving cryptographic standards | 0.4 | Mitigate | Hold the Crypto Boundary & Key-Management Architecture Review (Nov 2028), prototype the key chain on a ground testbed and baseline recovery procedures before PDR. | 1200 | 5500 | 14000 | 2200 | B–D1 | B–C | PDR slip or redesign of crypto boundary. | Adds security engineering cost. |
|  | 4 | R-004 | Spacecraft & Payload Technical | Radiation & Environment | Radiation-Induced Upset in Security Processors | Technical | Single-event upsets or latch-up in the security processors cause loss of authenticated control or forced re-keying more often than the availability budget allows. | Limited heritage; incomplete SEE test data | GEO electron and solar-particle environment during 2033–2040 | 0.35 | Mitigate | Perform heavy-ion and proton SEE testing, implement EDAC and watchdog-driven safe re-key, and verify recovery time against 99.5% availability. | 800 | 4200 | 12000 | 1470 | B–D1 | C–D1 | Service interruptions; possible redesign. | Adds radiation test and design cost. |
|  | 5 | R-005 | Spacecraft & Payload Technical | Thermal Control | High-Duty Payload Thermal Rejection Shortfall | Technical | The 2.8 m² radiator reference cannot reject ≥2.3 kW payload heat during eight-beam high-duty operations at end of life, forcing beam duty-cycle limits. | Payload dissipation growth; radiator area fixed by bus layout; optimistic EOL optical properties | Solstice hot cases; coating degradation in GEO | 0.4 | Mitigate | Carry ≥15% thermal margin at PDR, trade deployable radiator/heat-pipe options, and correlate the model after FM-1 thermal-vacuum. | 900 | 4800 | 13000 | 1920 | B–D1 | C–D1 | Duty-cycle limits reduce service capacity. | Affects thermal hardware and test cost. |
|  | 6 | R-006 | Ground Segment & Operations | Ground Software | Common-Mode Ground Software Failure | Technical | A defect common to the network-control software at both control sites causes simultaneous loss of beam management or protected-session authorization across all three spacecraft. | Single code base deployed at dual sites; limited independent verification | Third-party operating-system and library vulnerabilities; patch cycles | 0.3 | Mitigate | Stage deployments between sites, use dissimilar implementations for critical functions, apply independent V&V and maintain a manual fallback for key authorization. | 700 | 3500 | 9500 | 1050 | C–E | D2–E | Network-wide service outage. | Adds ground software and IV&V cost. |
|  | 7 | R-007 | Programmatic Integration & Execution | Schedule Planning & Control | Three-Unit Build Stagger Compression | Schedule | The 18-month unit AIT per flight model and the three-month stagger between FM-1 (Sep 2031), FM-2 and FM-3 (Mar 2032) leave little float before the Jan 2033 L1–L3 launch sequence; an anomaly on FM-1 propagates to FM-2 and FM-3. | Shared integration hall, test crews and GSE across three units; single payload qualification path; limited D1 float | Qelvoria Orbital Systems facility loading; Sorevex payload delivery cadence | 0.5 | Mitigate | Baseline a resource-loaded three-unit integration schedule, hold a dedicated FM-1 anomaly-recovery buffer, and pre-position second-shift test crews for FM-2/FM-3. | 2500 | 9000 | 22000 | 4500 | D1–D2 | D1–D2 | May slip SIR (May 2032), acceptance (Sep 2032) and one or more of the L1–L3 launches. | Drives D1/D2 standing-army cost and the worst-case (+2 mo/phase) schedule scenario. |
|  | 8 | R-008 | Programmatic Integration & Execution | Schedule Planning & Control | Launch Sequence Congestion (L1–L3 within Jan 2033) | Schedule | Three separate launches planned within a single month cannot be supported by one launch-campaign team, one LEOP network and one flight operations team without slipping at least one launch. | Single launch-campaign team; single LEOP operations team; shared ground support equipment | Lundrith Launch Consortium manifest; range availability at two launch sites | 0.5 | Mitigate | Stagger launches by at least 4–6 weeks in the realistic scenario, contract a second campaign team, and dry-run concurrent LEOP operations in simulation. | 1200 | 5200 | 14000 | 2600 | D2–LEOP | D2–E | May delay L2/L3 by 1–3 months and compress the six-month commissioning window. | Adds campaign, storage and LEOP-network cost; shifts the start of Phase E revenue/service. |
|  | 9 | R-009 | Programmatic Integration & Execution | Contracting & Procurement | Prime and Payload Contract Award Delay | Schedule | Award of the Qelvoria prime and Sorevex payload development contracts slips beyond Apr 2028 because of bid clarification, negotiation or evaluation delays after SRR. | Late SOW and security requirements; limited procurement staff; evaluation panel availability | Bidder clarification requests; approval cycles; possible bid protest | 0.45 | Mitigate | Release draft SOWs during Phase A studies, pre-clear evaluation criteria, and run negotiation in parallel with SRR close-out. | 600 | 2800 | 7500 | 1260 | A–B | B–C | Compresses the 14-month Phase B and pushes PDR beyond Mar 2029. | Increases PM/procurement LOE and Phase B duration uncertainty. |
|  | 10 | R-010 | Programmatic Integration & Execution | Contracting & Procurement | Launch Services Contract Terms for Three Vehicles | Programmatic | Launch contract for three separate GTO launches cannot secure firm dates, injection accuracy and re-flight terms at the planned price. | Late mass/envelope freeze; single-provider strategy | Launch market demand; Lundrith consortium manifest priorities | 0.35 | Mitigate | Award by Sep 2028 with firm manifest slots, injection-accuracy guarantees and re-flight/replacement options priced as options. | 1500 | 6500 | 18000 | 2275 | B–D2 | D2 | May raise launch service price or leave launch dates unprotected. | Widens launch-segment cost range; affects insurance assumptions. |
|  | 11 | R-011 | Programmatic Integration & Execution | Cost & Budget Management | Development Cost Growth | Cost | Actual development costs exceed the preliminary CADRe estimate because of design growth in the payload, reflectors and security processors, labour growth and additional test effort across three flight units. | Immature design at MCR; optimistic learning-curve assumptions for FM-2/FM-3; incomplete WBS | Supplier quotation changes; inflation; market conditions | 0.55 | Mitigate | Refresh bottoms-up estimates at SRR/PDR/CDR, maintain cost reserves, and reconcile growth with design maturity and Part C schedule scenarios. | 6000 | 22000 | 55000 | 12100 | A–D2 | B–D2 | May require additional funding, descoping or schedule changes. | Widens the cost uncertainty range and contingency requirement. |
|  | 12 | R-012 | Programmatic Integration & Execution | Cost & Budget Management | Recurring-Unit Learning Curve Not Realized | Cost | FM-2 and FM-3 do not achieve the assumed learning-curve savings because of design changes after FM-1 test anomalies and non-recurring rework. | Design changes after FM-1; turnover of AIT technicians | Supplier lot sizes too small for learning | 0.4 | Accept | Accept residual recurring-cost variance after freezing the FM-1 as-built configuration and applying a conservative 90% learning slope. | 1500 | 6000 | 15000 | 2400 | D1 | D1 | Recurring cost of FM-2/FM-3 higher than estimated. | Affects learning-curve CER and unit-cost assumptions. |
|  | 13 | R-013 | Programmatic Integration & Execution | Requirements & Scope Management | Requirements Maturity at SRR | Technical | Quantitative requirements for throughput, beam count, availability, key rotation and data retention remain unstable after SRR (Jan 2028). | Incomplete trade studies; inadequate traceability; late user input | User-community findings; spectrum coordination outcomes | 0.55 | Mitigate | Maintain requirement-to-design traceability, close mission trades before PDR, and baseline threshold/objective values with verification methods. | 1200 | 5500 | 14000 | 3025 | A–C | B–C | May cause redesign before or after PDR. | Materially affects cost, schedule and technical uncertainty. |
|  | 14 | R-014 | Programmatic Integration & Execution | Requirements & Scope Management | Service-Region and Beam Scope Growth | Programmatic | Authorized users request additional declared service regions, more than eight beams, or a higher availability target after the baseline is established. | Weak change control; ambiguous success criteria | Changing user demand; new operational theatres | 0.4 | Mitigate | Operate a formal change-control board and require quantified cost/schedule impacts; hold beam re-pointing as the primary response rather than new hardware. | 1500 | 6500 | 17000 | 2600 | B–D1 | B–D1 | May increase payload scope and development duration. | Requires additional engineering, payload and operations LOE. |
|  | 15 | R-015 | Programmatic Integration & Execution | Schedule Planning & Control | Schedule Margin Insufficient | Schedule | Integrated schedule margin between CDR (Mar 2030) and L1 (Jan 2033) is insufficient to absorb normal test anomalies on three units. | Compressed D1/D2; parallel builds | Supplier and launch provider availability | 0.5 | Mitigate | Hold explicit funded schedule reserve (1 month per year of development) and track margin burn-down monthly. | 2000 | 7500 | 18000 | 3750 | C–D2 | C–D2 | Launch dates slip; standing-army cost grows. | Supports realistic (+1 mo/phase) scenario. |
|  | 16 | R-016 | Programmatic Integration & Execution | Cost & Budget Management | Funding Gap Between Phases | Programmatic | Funding approval for Phase B/C or D does not arrive before the preceding phase ends, forcing contract slow-down or stop-work. | Late costing packages; incomplete business case | Central budget cycles; competing priorities | 0.35 | Accept | Accept after aligning the CADRe milestones with budget cycles and preparing bridge-funding options. | 1500 | 6000 | 16000 | 2100 | A–D1 | A–D1 | Contract slow-downs and re-mobilization costs. | Affects phasing of costs across fiscal years. |
|  | 17 | R-017 | Programmatic Integration & Execution | Governance Processes & Controls | Cost/Schedule Baseline Misalignment | Programmatic | The Part C phase schedule, contractor IMS and Part B cost phasing diverge, masking emerging variances. | Multiple unlinked tools; different phase nomenclatures (D1/D2 vs MAIT/SAIT) | Contractor reporting formats | 0.4 | Mitigate | Use the Part C phasing crosswalk as the single reference and require monthly EVM data mapped to it. | 300 | 1200 | 3000 | 480 | B–E | B–E | Late detection of overruns. | Reduces confidence in estimate-at-completion. |
|  | 18 | R-018 | Programmatic Integration & Execution | Governance Processes & Controls | Review Exit Criteria Not Met | Governance | PDR or CDR close with open actions on the crypto boundary, reflector deployment or thermal margins, carrying unresolved issues into D1. | Pressure to hold dates; inconsistent review criteria | Supplier data late | 0.4 | Mitigate | Define entrance/exit criteria per review and hold delta reviews rather than waiving criteria. | 800 | 3200 | 8500 | 1280 | B–D1 | B–D1 | Rework in D1 costs more than in C. | Increases uncertainty on D1 estimates. |
